96	    label stacks (indexed exactly like `brkB`/`contL`'s de Bruijn indices);
97	    `epi` is this function's epilogue label. `wf` guarantees index ranges and
98	    call arities; out-of-range lookups fall back to label 0 (never emitted for
99	    a wf program). -/
100	def lower (dat : Data) (brks conts : List Nat) (epi : Nat) : PStmt → M (List SymInstr)
101	  | .skip           => pure []
102	  | .annot _        => pure []
103	  | .seq a b        => do pure ((← lower dat brks conts epi a) ++ (← lower dat brks conts epi b))
104	  | .addi rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.addi T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
105	  | .add  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
106	                              ++ [.ins (.add T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
107	  | .sub  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
108	                              ++ [.ins (.sub T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
109	  | .orr  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
110	                              ++ [.ins (.or T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
111	  | .slli rd rs sh  => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.slli T0 T0 sh)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
112	  | .srli rd rs sh  => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.srli T0 T0 sh)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
113	  | .lbu  rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.lbu T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
114	  | .ld   rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.ld T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
115	  | .sb   rb rv imm => pure <| loadSlot rb T0 ++ loadSlot rv T1 ++ [.ins (.sb T0 T1 imm)]
116	  | .sd   rb rv imm => pure <| loadSlot rb T0 ++ loadSlot rv T1 ++ [.ins (.sd T0 T1 imm)]
117	  | .ife c a b t e  => do
118	      let lT ← fresh; let lEnd ← fresh
119	      let et ← lower dat brks conts epi t
120	      let ee ← lower dat brks conts epi e
121	      pure <| loadSlot a T0 ++ loadSlot b T1
122	           ++ [.br c T0 T1 lT] ++ ee ++ [.jmp lEnd, .label lT] ++ et ++ [.label lEnd]
123	  | .while c a b body => do
124	      let lTop ← fresh; let lBody ← fresh; let lEnd ← fresh
125	      let eb ← lower dat brks (lTop :: conts) epi body
126	      pure <| [.label lTop] ++ loadSlot a T0 ++ loadSlot b T1
127	           ++ [.br c T0 T1 lBody, .jmp lEnd, .label lBody]
128	           ++ eb ++ [.jmp lTop, .label lEnd]
129	  | .block body     => do
130	      let lEnd ← fresh
131	      let eb ← lower dat (lEnd :: brks) conts epi body
132	      pure <| eb ++ [.label lEnd]
13

23	structure State where
24	  reg : Nat → Word          -- x0..x31; x0 hardwired to 0 by rget/rset
25	  pc  : Word
26	  mem : Word → Byte
27	
28	/-- Read register `i` (x0 reads as 0). -/
29	@[inline] def State.rget (s : State) (i : Nat) : Word :=
30	  if i = 0 then 0 else s.reg i
31	
32	/-- Write register `i` (writes to x0 are ignored). -/
33	@[inline] def State.rset (s : State) (i : Nat) (v : Word) : State :=
34	  if i = 0 then s else { s with reg := fun j => if j = i then v else s.reg j }
35	
36	@[inline] def State.setPc (s : State) (p : Word) : State := { s with pc := p }
37	
38	@[inline] def State.loadByte (s : State) (a : Word) : Byte := s.mem a
39	
40	@[inline] def State.storeByte (s : State) (a : Word) (b : Byte) : State :=
41	  { s with mem := fun x => if x = a then b else s.mem x }
42	
43	/-- Fetch the little-endian 32-bit word at `pc`. -/
44	def fetch32 (s : State) : BitVec 32 :=
45	  let b0 := (s.mem s.pc).setWidth 32
46	  let b1 := (s.mem (s.pc + 1)).setWidth 32
47	  let b2 := (s.mem (s.pc + 2)).setWidth 32
48	  let b3 := (s.mem (s.pc + 3)).setWidth 32
49	  b0 ||| (b1 <<< 8) ||| (b2 <<< 16) ||| (b3 <<< 24)
50	
51	/-- Extract `len` bits starting at bit `lo` as a Nat. -/
52	@[inline] def field (w : BitVec 32) (lo len : Nat) : Nat :=
53	  (w.toNat >>> lo) &&& (2 ^ len - 1)
54	
55	inductive Instr where
56	  | addi (rd rs1 : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12)

<system-reminder>This memory is 29 days old. Memories are point-in-time observations, not live state — claims about code behavior or file:line citations may be outdated. Verify against current code before asserting as fact.</system-reminder>
1	---
2	name: lean-omega-classical-choice
3	description: "In this repo's Lean (4.30, no Mathlib), `omega` pulls in Classical.choice on compound goals — how to keep proofs choice-free"
4	metadata: 
5	  node_type: memory
6	  type: reference
7	  originSessionId: 22c92cdf-a670-4b69-84f5-d7c36a54d0b5
8	---
9	
10	In the hex0 Lean dev (Lean 4.30.0 via elan, **no Mathlib**), the kernel-checked
11	`core_refines` is `Classical.choice`-free (`#print axioms` = `[propext, Quot.sound]`).
12	Keeping it that way requires avoiding three leaks (the `∃ fuel` existential is NOT a
13	source — it's constructive):
14	
15	- **`omega` on a COMPOUND goal pulls in `Classical.choice`.** A conjunction goal
16	  (`a ≠ x ∧ a ≠ y`) or an iff goal (`p ↔ q`) makes `omega` use choice. Single
17	  (in)equality / `≠` goals, and disjunction/`≠`/conjunction *hypotheses*, are fine.
18	  Fix: split the goal first — `(repeat' apply And.intro) <;> omega` (arity-agnostic),
19	  or `⟨by omega, by omega⟩`. Also avoid feeding `omega` a `¬(P ∧ Q)` hypothesis
20	  (classical De Morgan): destruct with explicit `Nat.lt_or_ge` cases instead.
21	- **`decide_eq_decide`** pulls in choice. For `decide ((a:ℤ) < b) = decide (a < b)`
22	  use `simp only [Int.ofNat_lt]` instead.
23	- **`simp` on a base-case rewrite** can leak it (`skipComment_cons_nl` did). If the
24	  term is a closed numeral that computes, use `subst h; rfl`.
25	
26	The grammar layer (`Grammar.lean`) still uses `Classical.choice` (same patterns, not
27	yet cleaned). See [[task7-crosscheck-frontier]]. Build/check Lean via `~/.elan/bin` on
28	PATH; `lake env lean Hex0/AxCheck.lean` with a `#print axioms` to verify.
29	
```

## Phase 3 (finish): `StInv` slot algebra — DONE

**New file `lean/LowIR/ProgSim/SlotFacts.lean`** — the slot algebra `StInv` needs, all `[propext, Quot.sound]`:

- **Slot arithmetic**: `slotOff_add8_le_userOff` (a live reg's 8-byte slot fits the user-frame hole), `slotOff_disjoint` (distinct regs → 8-apart slots), `slotAddr_toNat` (no-wrap).
- **Slot read/write** (lifting WordMem): `loadWord_store_slot_same` (round-trip) and `loadWord_store_slot_ne` (a store to slot `r` leaves slot `r'≠r` untouched).
- **Blob preservation**: `fetch32_pc_congr`, `blobAddr_toNat`, `mem_storeWord_off_blob`, and `Installed_storeWord_off_blob` (a store off the code+data blob keeps the trusted fetch/data reads intact).
- **The payoff `StInv_store_slot`**: a machine `storeWord` at `sp + slotOff r` exactly mirrors the IL `rset r v`, re-establishing all six `StInv` conjuncts. The frame-/blob-placement side conditions (`hnw`/`hseg`/`hblob`/`hbd`) are explicit hypotheses — Phases 4/5 discharge them from `SimPre`.

**Two gotchas paid for** (both saved to memory):
- Register binders must be typed `Nat`, not `Reg` — a `≤` stated at `Reg = abbrev Nat` is invisible to `omega`.
- **WordMem axiom-hygiene retrofit**: `byte_bit`/`loadWord_storeWord_same` were silently pulling `Classical.choice` (the last commit's stale olean hid it). `omega` on iff/∧/**∨** goals *and* `by_cases` all route through `Classical`. Fixed by splitting to atoms first (`constructor`/`repeat' apply And.intro`/`exfalso`), replacing the window-tiling `omega` with an explicit constructive `window_tiling`, and `by_cases`→`cases Nat.decLe`. Now `[propext, Quot.sound]`.

Full project build green (34 jobs, only the two expected `sorry`s: the historical T1 and `prog_sim`). Committed as `6a4fbb2`; RESUME-PROGSIM.md, the campaign-frontier and omega-Classical memories updated.

**Next** (per the plan): the **vertical slice** (§8.3 / Phase 4.1) — the go/no-go for the relation design: state `lower_sim`/`Emitted`, prove only the skip/annot/ops cases, `sorry` the rest, and check a toy straight-line `prog_sim` (sub3-class) against the differential oracle.

220	def symSize : SymInstr → Nat
221	  | .label _ => 0
222	  | .cref _  => 20
223	  | _        => 4

227	def layoutItems : List SymInstr → Nat → List (Nat × SymInstr) × List (Nat × Nat) × Nat
228	  | [], pos => ([], [], pos)

262	      let hi : Int := (δ - lo) / 4096
263	      if -2048 ≤ hi && hi ≤ 2047 then

265	              .addi T1 0 (BitVec.ofInt 12 hi), .slli T1 T1 12,
266	              .addi T1 T1 (BitVec.ofInt 12 lo),

459	            .seq (.call 1 1 "frameLocal" #v[11] #v[12]) <|   -- x12 := frameLocal(x11)
460	            (.addi 10 12 1) }                                -- ret := x12+1
461	
462	/-- Early `ret` via block/brk interplay: returns 7 out of a nested block. -/
463	def early : FunDef :=
464	  { argc := 0, rvc := 1, params := #v[], rets := #v[10]
465	    frameSize := 0, frameReg := 5
466	    body := .seq (.block (.seq (.addi 10 0 7) (.seq .ret (.addi 10 0 9))))
467	                 (.addi 10 0 3) }   -- unreachable: ret escapes the block
468	
469	/-- Hog: frameSize bigger than the whole stack — must trip the overflow check. -/
470	def hog : FunDef :=
471	  { argc := 0, rvc := 0, params := #v[], rets := #v[]
472	    frameSize := 0x10000, frameReg := 8, body := .skip }
473	
474	def testEnv : Env :=
475	  [("sub3", sub3), ("sumTo", sumTo), ("frameLocal", frameLocal),
476	   ("caller", caller), ("early", early), ("hog", hog),
477	   ("hogCaller", { argc := 0, rvc := 0, params := #v[], rets := #v[]
478	                   frameSize := 0, frameReg := 5
479	                   body := .call 0 0 "hog" #v[] #v[] })]
480	
481	def STACK_LO : Word := 0x4000
482	def SP0      : Word := 0x8000
483	
484	def testRun (f : Name) (args : List Word) : Option St :=
485	  run testEnv STACK_LO 1000 f args zeroMem SP0
486	
487	-- the whole test env is well-formed
488	#guard wfEnv testEnv
489	
490	-- arithmetic: (30+12)-2 = 40
491	#guard (testRun "sub3" [30, 12, 2]).map (·.rget 10 |>.toNat) = some 40
492	
493	-- loop: sum 1..10 = 55; sum 1..0 = 0
494	#guard (testRun "sumTo" [10]).map (·.rget 10 |>.toNat) = some 55
495	#guard (testRun "sumTo" [0]).map (·.rget 10 |>.toNat) = some 0
496	
497	-- frame local sd/ld roundtrip
498	#guard (testRun "frameLocal" [0xDEAD]).map (·.rget 10 |>.toNat) = some 0xDEAD
499

1	/-
2	  LowIR.Compile — Prog → RV64I instructions (EXECUTABLE, UNVERIFIED first cut).
3	
4	  Strategy: MEMORY-LOCALS (-O0). Every IL register gets an 8-byte slot in the
5	  function's machine frame; no register allocation. Correctness-first — the
6	  point is a differential-testing target for the D7/D8 semantics, and later the
7	  subject of `compile_sim`-style theorems (performance is a non-goal, N9).
8	
9	  Machine frame (grows down; all offsets sp-relative, sp = x2 after prologue):
10	
11	        sp + 0                  : saved ra
12	        sp + 8*(1+r)            : slot for IL register r   (r = 0..maxReg)
13	        sp + 8*(maxReg+2)       : user frame (frameSize bytes) — the address
14	                                  handed to the IL `frameReg`
15	        total = 8*(maxReg+2) + frameSize   (must fit imm12: ≤ 2000, checked)
16	
17	  Physical registers: x2=sp, x1=ra, x5/x6 = t0/t1 scratch, x10..x17 = a0..a7
18	  argument/return marshalling (so argc,rvc ≤ 8, checked). Nothing is live
19	  across an IL statement except the slots, so calls need no caller-save logic.
20	
21	  IL zero-init is matched EXACTLY: the prologue `sd x0` into every non-param,
22	  non-frameReg slot (differential tests stay honest; definite-assignment
23	  optimization comes later).
24	
25	  Pipeline: lower to `SymInstr` (labels symbolic, calls by name) → lay out the
26	  whole program (all instructions are 4 bytes) → resolve branches/jumps/calls
27	  to relative offsets (range-checked) → `Rv64i.Instr` list. Encoding to bytes
28	  reuses `LowIR.encode`/`asmBytes`. Entry convention: the program starts with
29	  a single `jal ra, <entry>` stub at `codeBase`; when the entry function
30	  returns, pc = codeBase+4 — run the machine with `runFuel (codeBase+4)`.
31	-/
32	import LowIR.Prog
33	
34	namespace LowIR.Compile
35	
36	open LowIR (Cond condInstr jal0)
37	open LowIR.Prog (Reg Name FunDef Env Data Program wfProgram pad8 dataOffsetsFrom dataSegment)
38	open Rv64i (Word Byte Instr)
39	
40	/-- The IL being compiled (the parent `LowIR.Stmt` also exists — be explicit). -/
41	local notation "PStmt" => LowIR.Prog.Stmt
42	
43	/-! ### Physical register roles -/
44	
45	def SP : Reg := 2
46	def RA : Reg := 1
47	def T0 : Reg := 5
48	def T1 : Reg := 6
49	/-- Argument/return register `a_i` = x10+i. -/
50	def A (i : Nat) : Reg := 10 + i
51	
52	/-! ### Symbolic instructions -/
53	
54	/-- Position-independent instruction stream: concrete instructions, label
55	    markers (emit nothing), and label/name-relative control transfers. -/
56	inductive SymInstr where
57	  | ins   (i : Instr)                          -- concrete, position-independent
58	  | label (l : Nat)                            -- marker (0 bytes)
59	  | br    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (l : Nat)     -- branch (phys regs) to label
60	  | jmp   (l : Nat)                            -- jal x0, label
```

@BitVec.signExtend : {w : Nat} → (v : Nat) → BitVec w → BitVec v
def BitVec.signExtend : {w : Nat} → (v : Nat) → BitVec w → BitVec v :=
fun {w} v x => BitVec.ofInt v x.toInt
@BitVec.toInt_ofNat : ∀ {n : Nat} (x : Nat), (OfNat.ofNat x).toInt = (↑x).bmod (2 ^ n)
BitVec.ofInt_natCast : ∀ (w n : Nat), BitVec.ofInt w ↑n = BitVec.ofNat w n

    If the IL says `stmt` runs `s ↦ s'` with a `.normal` outcome, and the machine
    sits at `codeBase + pos` in a `StInv`-related state with `emit stmt` installed
    there, then it runs `k` steps to `codeBase + (pos + 4·|emit stmt|)` in a state
    still `StInv`-related to `s'`. The `.normal`-only form covers the straight-line
    slice (skip/annot/arith/seq); the control-flow cases (whose outcome selects a
    label target) and the `ld/sd/cref/clen` memory cases are the `sorry`'d
    remainder (Phases 4.2–4.4). The frame-/blob-placement hypotheses
    (`hframe`/`hnw`/`hseg`/`hblob`/`hbd`) are `SlotFacts`' side conditions, from
    `fnOk`+`SimPre` downstream. -/
theorem lower_sim
    {P : Program} {dbase : Name → Option Word} {pad : Name → Nat} {stackLo : Word}
    {L : Layout} {fd : FunDef} {holes : List Hole}
    (fuel : Nat) (stmt : PStmt) (s s' : St) (m : State) (pos : Nat)
    (hexec : LowIR.Prog.exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel stmt s = some (s', .normal))
    (hinv  : StInv L fd holes s m)
    (hpc   : m.pc = L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 pos)
    (hem   : Emitted L pos (emit stmt))
    (hreg  : maxRegS stmt ≤ maxRegF fd)
    (hframe : userOff fd ≤ 2000)
    (hnw   : s.sp.toNat + userOff fd ≤ 2 ^ 64)
    (hseg  : 4 * L.instrs.length ≤ L.segStart)
```

    (m.rset a v).rget b = m.rget b := by
  unfold State.rset State.rget; split
  · rfl

/-- The fetch bridge for an intermediate machine state `mj` reached mid-op: same
    code memory as `m`, positioned at instruction `j`. -/
theorem decode_at (L : Layout) (m mj : State) (pos : Nat) (is : List Instr)
    (hem : Emitted L pos is) (hinst : Installed L m) (j : Nat) (hj : j < is.length)
    (hpcj : mj.pc = L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (pos + 4 * j)) (hmemj : mj.mem = m.mem) :
    decode (fetch32 mj) = is[j]'hj := by
  have : fetch32 mj = fetch32 { m with pc := L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (pos + 4 * j) } := by

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  17:42:45; model 15.9s

Everything is green and committed. Here's the summary of this session.

## Vertical slice: **GO** — the `StInv` relation design is validated

I started the Phase 4.1 vertical slice (the go/no-go checkpoint for the whole `compile_sim`-for-Prog campaign) and got it to a clean **PASS**: a real arithmetic instruction now simulates end-to-end against the trusted machine `step`.

### What landed (`lean/LowIR/ProgSim/StmtSim.lean`, 3 commits)

**The relation, stated and validated:**
- `emit` — the resolved straight-line lowering; `#guard`'d to equal the real `Compile.lower` of `sub3.body` (executable oracle, per the discipline).
- `Emitted L pos is` — static "code segment carries `is` at byte `pos`", with append-split lemmas.
- `decode_emitted`/`decode_at` — the fetch bridge: `Emitted` + `Installed` (a `StInv` conjunct, preserved across steps) ⇒ the machine decodes the right instruction.

**The machinery (all `[propext, Quot.sound]`):**
- `stepN`/`stepN_add` — own machine iteration (`f^[n]`/`Nat.iterate` are Mathlib-only here).
- `step_{addi,add,sub,or,slli,srli,ld,sd}` — one decoded instruction ⇒ one `step`.
- `signExtend_ofNat_lt` — slot immediates sign-extend to themselves, clean via `toInt_eq_toNat_bmod` (stdlib's `toNat_signExtend` is `Classical.choice`-tainted).
- `StInv_congr`/`StInv_scratch` — `StInv` reads `m` only through x2 and memory, so scratch-reg writes and pc bumps preserve it.
- `load_step` (reads a slot via `StInv`, uniform over `r=0`), `pc_add4`.

**`lower_sim`** (the `.normal`-outcome simulation, by fuel induction): **skip/annot + `addi` (rs, rd ≠ 0) proven end-to-end.** The `addi` case composes every piece — slot read, decode bridge, scratch preservation, the P1 slot store (`SlotFacts.StInv_store_slot`), pc arithmetic — showing the plain-equality relation holds across a real ld/addi/sd sequence. **The design works.**

### Also this session
- **Phase 3 finish** (`SlotFacts.lean`): the full StInv slot algebra + `StInv_store_slot`.
- **WordMem axiom-hygiene retrofit**: `byte_bit`/round-trip were silently pulling `Classical.choice` (stale-olean hid it); cleaned to `[propext, Quot.sound]` — discovered omega on iff/∧/**∨** goals *and* `by_cases` all route through `Classical` (saved to memory).

Full build green; remaining `sorry`s are the addi r=0 subcases, other ops, seq, control-flow/memory (mechanical variations / later phases) plus the pre-existing `prog_sim` and historical T1. Next step (in the RESUME doc + memory): finish `lower_sim` — r=0 subcases, the other ops, then **seq** (the composition case via `Emitted_append_*` + `stepN_add` + the IH).

def emit : PStmt → List Instr
  | .skip            => []
  | .annot _         => []
  | .addi rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.addi T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .add  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.add T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .sub  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.sub T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .orr  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.or  T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .slli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.slli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .srli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.srli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .seq a b         => emit a ++ emit b
  | _                => []

/-! ## `Emitted` — the static "code segment carries `is` at `pos`" fact. -/

/-- `L.instrs` has `is` as its segment starting at byte offset `pos`
    (`pos` 4-aligned; each `is[j]` is `L.instrs[pos/4 + j]`). Static in `L`; the
    machine fetch is recovered from `Installed` (a `StInv` conjunct). -/
def Emitted (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is : List Instr) : Prop :=
  pos % 4 = 0 ∧ ∀ (j : Nat) (h : j < is.length),
    ∃ (h2 : pos / 4 + j < L.instrs.length), L.instrs[pos / 4 + j] = is[j]'h

/-- `Emitted` of a concatenation splits: `is₁` at `pos`, `is₂` at `pos + 4|is₁|`. -/
theorem Emitted_append_left (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is₁ is₂ : List Instr)
    (h : Emitted L pos (is₁ ++ is₂)) : Emitted L pos is₁ := by
  obtain ⟨ha, hf⟩ := h
  refine ⟨ha, fun j hj => ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨h2, he⟩ := hf j (by simp only [List.length_append]; omega)
  exact ⟨h2, by rw [he, List.getElem_append_left hj]⟩

theorem Emitted_append_right (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is₁ is₂ : List Instr)
    (h : Emitted L pos (is₁ ++ is₂)) : Emitted L (pos + 4 * is₁.length) is₂ := by
  obtain ⟨ha, hf⟩ := h
  refine ⟨by omega, fun j hj => ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨h2, he⟩ := hf (is₁.length + j) (by simp only [List.length_append]; omega)
  have hpos : (pos + 4 * is₁.length) / 4 + j = pos / 4 + (is₁.length + j) := by omega
...
</persisted-output>

--- def body ---
def maxRegS : PStmt → Nat
  | .skip | .annot _ | .ret | .brkB _ | .contL _ => 0
  | .seq a b          => max (maxRegS a) (maxRegS b)
  | .addi rd rs _     => max rd rs
  | .add  rd r1 r2    => max rd (max r1 r2)
  | .sub  rd r1 r2    => max rd (max r1 r2)
  | .orr  rd r1 r2    => max rd (max r1 r2)
  | .slli rd rs _     => max rd rs
  | .srli rd rs _     => max rd rs
  | .lbu  rd rs _     => max rd rs
  | .ld   rd rs _     => max rd rs
  | .sb   rb rv _     => max rb rv
  | .sd   rb rv _     => max rb rv
  | .ife _ a b t e    => max (max a b) (max (maxRegS t) (maxRegS e))
  | .while _ a b body => max (max a b) (maxRegS body)
  | .block body       => maxRegS body
  | .call _ _ _ args rets =>
      max (args.toList.foldl max 0) (rets.toList.foldl max 0)
  | .cref rd _        => rd
  | .clen rd _        => rd

lean/LowIR/Compile.lean

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  18:02:29; tool 93ms

```
structure St where
  regs : Reg → Word
  mem  : Word → Byte
  sp   : Word

@[inline] def St.rget (s : St) (i : Reg) : Word := if i = 0 then 0 else s.regs i

@[inline] def St.rset (s : St) (i : Reg) (v : Word) : St :=
  if i = 0 then s else { s with regs := fun j => if j = i then v else s.regs j }

@[inline] def St.loadByte (s : St) (a : Word) : Byte := s.mem a

@[inline] def St.storeByte (s : St) (a : Word) (b : Byte) : St :=
  { s with mem := fun x => if x = a then b else s.mem x }

/-- Little-endian 64-bit load — byte order identical to `Rv64i.State.loadWord`. -/
def St.loadWord (s : St) (a : Word) : Word :=
  (s.mem a).setWidth 64 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 1)).setWidth 64) <<< 8 |||

373:def StInv (L : Layout) (fd : FunDef) (holes : List Hole) (s : St) (m : State) : Prop :=
def StInv (L : Layout) (fd : FunDef) (holes : List Hole) (s : St) (m : State) : Prop :=
  m.rget 2 = s.sp
  ∧ (∀ r, 1 ≤ r → r ≤ maxRegF fd →
        s.rget r = m.loadWord (s.sp + BitVec.ofNat 64 (slotOff r)))
  ∧ Installed L m
  ∧ (∀ a, ¬ MachPriv L holes a → s.mem a = m.mem a)
  ∧ holes.head? = some (s.sp, userOff fd)
  ∧ s.sp.toNat % 8 = 0

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  18:07:15; model 14.3s

All done. Here's what landed for "finish the remaining `lower_sim` ops."

## The straight-line `lower_sim` fragment is complete

`lower_sim` now proves **skip, annot, all six arithmetic ops, and seq** — every register value including 0, no remaining `sorry` in that fragment. Only control-flow and memory cases (Phases 4.2–4.4) are still stubbed.

### The key move: factor, don't grind

Rather than 6 ops × 4 register-casing branches (~480 lines), I built four reusable, `[propext, Quot.sound]`-clean simulators so **source registers never get cased** and the `rd=0`/`rd≠0` split lives in exactly one place:

- **`run_load`** — one `loadSlotI` segment, uniform over `r=0`; `StInv` preserved, `pc+4`, memory + other registers untouched.
- **`run_store`** — the store tail: `rd=0` is a no-op (`rset 0` discards, 0 steps), `rd≠0` is `sd` + `StInv_store_slot` (the P1 payoff). The only `rd` case-split in the whole file.
- **`single_op_sim`** / **`two_op_sim`** — the `load(s)→compute→store` shape with the compute instruction `C` and result value abstract; each op supplies its one-instruction `step_*` lemma as `hC`.

The old 80-line `addi` proof (which only covered `rs,rd≠0`) collapsed to **11 lines covering every register value**. `add`/`sub`/`orr`/`slli`/`srli` are the same one-liner shape.

### `seq` — the composition go/no-go

Chains the fuel induction hypothesis twice: `a` must finish `.normal` (any other outcome would surface as the seq's outcome — the three non-normal branches close by `absurd`), then `ih a → s1 → ih b → s'`, assembled with `stepN_add` (`k = k₁+k₂`). The frame side conditions transfer to the intermediate `s1` via new **`StInv_sp_eq`**: both invariants pin `holes.head? = some(·.sp, userOff fd)` over the same `holes`, so `s1.sp = s.sp` falls out — no separate sp-preservation lemma.

**Verified:** all simulators `#print axioms` = `[propext, Quot.sound]` (no `Classical`); full `lake build` green; the sole `lower_sim` `sorry` is the deferred control-flow/memory `all_goals`.

**Commits:** `e688c5a` (6 ops), `dade03c` (seq), plus doc/memory updates. Next in the plan: the toy `prog_sim`/sub3 corollary — sub3 is skip/arith/seq only, now entirely within the proven fragment.

364	def MachStack (stackLo sp0 : Word) (a : Word) : Prop :=
365	  memRange a stackLo (sp0.toNat - stackLo.toNat)
366	
367	/-- **`StInv L fd holes s m`** — the per-statement simulation invariant
368	    (RESUME-PROGSIM §3.1). At every IL statement boundary during `fd`'s body:
369	    `sp ≡ x2`; each live IL register sits in its 8-byte machine slot at
370	    `sp + slotOff r`; the code+data is installed; IL and machine memory agree
371	    off the machine-private bytes; and the current activation's hole is
372	    `[sp, sp + userOff fd)` with `sp` 8-aligned. -/
373	def StInv (L : Layout) (fd : FunDef) (holes : List Hole) (s : St) (m : State) : Prop :=
374	  m.rget 2 = s.sp
375	  ∧ (∀ r, 1 ≤ r → r ≤ maxRegF fd →
376	        s.rget r = m.loadWord (s.sp + BitVec.ofNat 64 (slotOff r)))
377	  ∧ Installed L m
378	  ∧ (∀ a, ¬ MachPriv L holes a → s.mem a = m.mem a)
379	  ∧ holes.head? = some (s.sp, userOff fd)
380	  ∧ s.sp.toNat % 8 = 0
381

383	    sp0)` is well-formed and disjoint from the code+data blob, and `sp0` is
384	    8-aligned. (The per-function stack budget is SUBSUMED by P1's overflow
385	    check, so it is NOT a hypothesis here — that is the point of §2.) -/
386	structure SimPre (L : Layout) (stackLo sp0 : Word) : Prop where
387	  spAligned    : sp0.toNat % 8 = 0
388	  stackNonEmpty : stackLo.toNat ≤ sp0.toNat

=== sub3 FunDef ===
/-- Test fixture: `sub3 (a,b,c) = (a+b)-c`, no frame. Params x10 x11 x12 → ret x10. -/
def sub3 : FunDef :=
  { argc := 3, rvc := 1, params := #v[10, 11, 12], rets := #v[10]
    frameSize := 0, frameReg := 5
    body := .seq (.add 6 10 11) (.sub 10 6 12) }

/-- `sumTo (n) = 1+2+…+n` via a while loop with continue-free body. x10=n → x10. -/
def sumTo : FunDef :=
  { argc := 1, rvc := 1, params := #v[10], rets := #v[10]
    frameSize := 0, frameReg := 5
    body := .seq (.addi 6 0 0) <|                    -- acc := 0
            .seq (.addi 7 0 1) <|                    -- i := 1
            .seq (.while .geu 10 7                   -- while n ≥u i  (i counts up)
                   (.seq (.add 6 6 7) (.addi 7 7 1)))
                 (.addi 10 6 0) }                    -- ret := acc

/-- `frameLocal (v) : write v to the frame local at frameReg+0 via sd, read it
    back via ld, return it` — the D8 ld/sd/frame roundtrip. x8 = frame base. -/
def frameLocal : FunDef :=
  { argc := 1, rvc := 1, params := #v[10], rets := #v[10]
    frameSize := 16, frameReg := 8
    body := .seq (.sd 8 10 0) <|                     -- mem64[frame+0] := v
            .seq (.addi 10 0 0) <|                   -- clobber x10
            (.ld 10 8 0) }                           -- x10 := mem64[frame+0]

/-- `caller (v) = frameLocal(v+1) + 1`, i.e. v+2 — a two-function call. -/
def caller : FunDef :=
  { argc := 1, rvc := 1, params := #v[10], rets := #v[10]
    frameSize := 0, frameReg := 5
    body := .seq (.addi 11 10 1) <|                          -- x11 := v+1
            .seq (.call 1 1 "frameLocal" #v[11] #v[12]) <|   -- x12 := frameLocal(x11)
            (.addi 10 12 1) }                                -- ret := x12+1

=== maxRegF def ===
def maxRegF (fd : FunDef) : Nat :=
  max (maxRegS fd.body) <|
  max fd.frameReg <|
  max (fd.params.toList.foldl max 0) (fd.rets.toList.foldl max 0)

def userOff (fd : FunDef) : Nat := 8 * (maxRegF fd + 2)

open LowIR (Cond evalCond)

open Rv64i (Word Byte)

variable (P : Program) (dbase : Name → Option Word) (pad : Name → Nat) (stackLo : Word)

/-! ### Leaf statements — pure value/outcome equations. -/

theorem exec_skip (fuel : Nat) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) .skip s = some (s, .normal) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_annot (fuel : Nat) (a : String) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.annot a) s = some (s, .normal) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_addi (fuel rd rs : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.addi rd rs imm) s
      = some (s.rset rd (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64), .normal) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_add (fuel rd r1 r2 : Nat) (s : St) :

Done. The sub3 `prog_sim` corollary is complete and the vertical-slice go/no-go is now **fully closed, `sorry`-free**.

## What landed (`StmtSim.lean`, commit `ab622b6`)

**`sub3_body_exec`** — the IL spec: `sub3.body = (add 6 10 11; sub 10 6 12)` computes `(a+b)−c` into register x10, proved forward through the `exec_*` unfolders.

**`sub3_body_sim`** — the end-to-end simulation: from a `StInv`-related machine state with `emit sub3.body` installed, the machine runs `k` steps to a `StInv`-related state for `s'` whose return register x10 holds `(a+b)−c` — sub3's spec.

**The key decision:** I proved it by driving the `add` then the `sub` through the fully-proven **`two_op_sim` directly**, *not* through `lower_sim`. That matters — `lower_sim` still carries `sorryAx` from its stubbed control-flow cases, and Lean's axiom tracking is coarse: any user of `lower_sim` inherits `sorry` even on branches (like sub3's) that are fully proven. Bypassing it makes the corollary genuinely **`[propext, Quot.sound]`** (verified via `#print axioms`).

**Oracle tie:** a `native_decide` example confirms the `(a+b)−c` formula evaluates to `40` at inputs `[30,12,2]` — exactly what `diff_sub3` checks. So the symbolic simulation reproduces the differential oracle's concrete number.

This is precisely the checkpoint the plan called for (§Phase 4.1): *"if the relation is wrong, it is wrong HERE, cheaply, against the differential oracle."* It holds.

A few gotchas worth noting (saved to memory): the `exec_*` unfolders take `P/dbase/pad/stackLo` explicitly (section `variable`s); `St.rget`/`St.rset` are `@[inline]` and resist `simp`/`unfold`, but concrete-index reads like `(s.rset 6 v).rget 6 = v` and the intermediate `sp` preservation hold by `rfl` (defeq); the final pc-length equation closes with `pc_congr _ rfl`.

Full build green (only the 3 expected `sorry`s: `lower_sim`'s control-flow `all_goals`, `Defs.prog_sim`, and the historical hex1 T1). Committed with docs + memory updated. Next per the plan: Phase 4.2 memory ops (`ld/sd/lbu/sb`).

=== exec unfolders for memory ops ===
theorem exec_lbu (fuel rd rs : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.lbu rd rs imm) s
      = some (s.rset rd ((s.loadByte (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64)).setWidth 64), .normal) := by
  simp [exec]

theorem exec_sb (fuel rb rv : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.sb rb rv imm) s
      = some (s.storeByte (s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64) ((s.rget rv).setWidth 8), .normal) := by
  simp [exec]

theorem exec_ld (fuel rd rs : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.ld rd rs imm) s
      = some (s.rset rd (s.loadWord (s.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64)), .normal) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_sd (fuel rb rv : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.sd rb rv imm) s
      = some (s.storeWord (s.rget rb + imm.signExtend 64) (s.rget rv), .normal) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_brkB (fuel k : Nat) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.brkB k) s = some (s, .brk k) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_contL (fuel k : Nat) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.contL k) s = some (s, .cont k) := by simp [exec]

theorem exec_ret (fuel : Nat) (s : St) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) .ret s = some (s, .ret) := by simp [exec]

/-! ### Const-data references — conditioned on the address/length lookups. -/

theorem exec_cref (fuel rd : Nat) (d : Name) (s : St) {a : Word} (h : dbase d = some a) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.cref rd d) s = some (s.rset rd a, .normal) := by
  simp [exec, h]

theorem exec_clen (fuel rd : Nat) (d : Name) (s : St) {bs : List Byte}
    (h : List.lookup d P.data = some bs) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.clen rd d) s
      = some (s.rset rd (BitVec.ofNat 64 bs.length), .normal) := by
  simp [exec, h]

/-! ### `ife` — collapse to the taken branch. -/

theorem exec_ife_then (fuel : Nat) (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (t e : Stmt) (s : St)
    (h : evalCond c (s.rget a) (s.rget b) = true) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.ife c a b t e) s = exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel t s := by
  simp [exec, h]

theorem exec_ife_else (fuel : Nat) (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (t e : Stmt) (s : St)
    (h : evalCond c (s.rget a) (s.rget b) = false) :
    exec P dbase pad stackLo (fuel+1) (.ife c a b t e) s = exec P dbase pad stackLo fuel e s := by
  simp [exec, h]

/-! ### `seq` — threads on `normal`, short-circuits otherwise. -/

=== step lemmas (ld/sd/lbu/sb) in StmtSim ===
theorem step_ld (m : State) (rd rs : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .ld rd rs imm) :
    step m = (m.rset rd (m.loadWord (m.rget rs + imm.signExtend 64))).setPc (m.pc + 4) := by
  simp only [step, h]

theorem step_sd (m : State) (rs1 rs2 : Nat) (imm : BitVec 12)
    (h : decode (fetch32 m) = .sd rs1 rs2 imm) :
    step m = (m.storeWord (m.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64) (m.rget rs2)).setPc (m.pc + 4) := by
  simp only [step, h]

    this stdlib, this route stays `[propext, Quot.sound]`. -/

```
=== machine step lbu/sb/ld/sd (Rv64i.lean) ===
  let next := s.pc + 4
  match decode (fetch32 s) with
  | .addi rd rs1 imm => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64)).setPc next
  | .add  rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 + s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .sub  rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 - s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .or   rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 ||| s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .slli rd rs1 sh  => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 <<< sh)).setPc next
  | .srli rd rs1 sh  => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 >>> sh)).setPc next
  | .lbu  rd rs1 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.rset rd ((s.loadByte a).setWidth 64)).setPc next
  | .ld   rd rs1 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.rset rd (s.loadWord a)).setPc next
  | .sb   rs1 rs2 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.storeByte a ((s.rget rs2).setWidth 8)).setPc next
  | .sd   rs1 rs2 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.storeWord a (s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .beq  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if s.rget rs1 = s.rget rs2 then s.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else next)
  | .blt  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if (s.rget rs1).slt (s.rget rs2) then s.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else next)
  | .bge  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if (s.rget rs1).slt (s.rget rs2) then next else s.pc + imm.signExtend 64)
=== St.loadWord/storeWord bodies (Prog.lean 108-130) ===
def St.loadWord (s : St) (a : Word) : Word :=
  (s.mem a).setWidth 64 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 1)).setWidth 64) <<< 8 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 2)).setWidth 64) <<< 16 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 3)).setWidth 64) <<< 24 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 4)).setWidth 64) <<< 32 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 5)).setWidth 64) <<< 40 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 6)).setWidth 64) <<< 48 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 7)).setWidth 64) <<< 56

/-- Little-endian 64-bit store — identical to `Rv64i.State.storeWord`. -/
def St.storeWord (s : St) (a : Word) (v : Word) : St :=
  (((((((s.storeByte a (v.setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 1) ((v >>> 8).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 2) ((v >>> 16).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 3) ((v >>> 24).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 4) ((v >>> 32).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 5) ((v >>> 40).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 6) ((v >>> 48).setWidth 8)
    ).storeByte (a + 7) ((v >>> 56).setWidth 8)

/-- Build the callee's entry state (D7/D8): check stack overflow against
    `stackLo`, fresh zero registers with params bound to the argument VALUES,
    frame base in `frameReg` (binds after params — `wf` keeps them disjoint),
    callee `sp` dropped by `frameSize` PLUS the padding oracle `pad`, caller's
```

339:def memRange (a base : Word) (len : Nat) : Prop :=

def memRange (a base : Word) (len : Nat) : Prop :=
  base.toNat ≤ a.toNat ∧ a.toNat < base.toNat + len
def memRangeB (a base : Word) (len : Nat) : Bool :=
  base.toNat ≤ a.toNat && a.toNat < base.toNat + len

def Installed (L : Layout) (m : State) : Prop :=
  (∀ (j : Nat) (h : j < L.instrs.length),
      Rv64i.decode (Rv64i.fetch32 { m with pc := L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (4 * j) })
        = L.instrs[j]'h)
  ∧ (∀ (i : Nat) (h : i < (dataSegment L.data).length),
      m.mem (L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (L.segStart + i)) = (dataSegment L.data)[i]'h)

/-! ### Computable shadows of `Installed` (the #guard oracle). -/

/-- Every instruction slot decodes correctly (computable form of `Installed`'s
    code conjunct). -/
def codeInstalledB (L : Layout) (m : State) : Bool :=
  (List.range L.instrs.length).all fun j =>
    Rv64i.decode (Rv64i.fetch32 { m with pc := L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (4 * j) })
      == L.instrs.getD j .unknown

/-- Every data byte is present (computable form of `Installed`'s data conjunct). -/
def dataInstalledB (L : Layout) (m : State) : Bool :=
  (List.range (dataSegment L.data).length).all fun i =>
    m.mem (L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 (L.segStart + i))
      == (dataSegment L.data).getD i 0

381	    (s : St) (m : State) (rd rs pos : Nat) (C : Instr) (vC : Word)
382	    (hinv : StInv L fd holes s m) (hpc : m.pc = L.codeBase + BitVec.ofNat 64 pos)
383	    (hem : Emitted L pos (loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [C] ++ storeSlotI rd T0))

386	    (hnw : s.sp.toNat + userOff fd ≤ 2 ^ 64) (hseg : 4 * L.instrs.length ≤ L.segStart)
387	    (hblob : L.codeBase.toNat + L.blobLen ≤ 2 ^ 64)
388	    (hbd : L.codeBase.toNat + L.blobLen ≤ s.sp.toNat
389	             ∨ s.sp.toNat + userOff fd ≤ L.codeBase.toNat)
390	    (hC : ∀ m', decode (fetch32 m') = C → m'.rget T0 = s.rget rs →
391	            step m' = (m'.rset T0 vC).setPc (m'.pc + 4)) :
392	    ∃ k, StInv L fd holes (s.rset rd vC) (stepN k m)
393	       ∧ (stepN k m).pc = L.codeBase
394	           + BitVec.ofNat 64 (pos + 4 * (loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [C] ++ storeSlotI rd T0).length) := by
395	  have hinst : Installed L m := hinv.2.2.1
396	  -- instruction 0: load rs into T0
397	  have hemL : Emitted L pos (loadSlotI rs T0) :=
398	    Emitted_append_left L pos _ _ (Emitted_append_left L pos _ _ hem)
399	  obtain ⟨hinv1, h1pc, h1mem, h1T0, -⟩ :=
400	    run_load L fd holes s m rs T0 pos hinv hrs hfrs (by decide) (by decide) hpc hemL
401	  -- instruction 1: the compute `C` at pos+4
402	  have hemC : Emitted L (pos + 4) [C] := by
403	    have h := Emitted_append_right L pos (loadSlotI rs T0) [C] (Emitted_append_left L pos _ _ hem)

406	    have h := decode_at L m (step m) (pos + 4) [C] hemC hinst 0 (by simp) (by rw [h1pc]) h1mem

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  19:01:23; model 13.9s

All four memory ops are done. Here's the summary of "do the lower_sim memory ops."

## Phase 4.2 complete — `ld`/`sd`/`lbu`/`sb` all proven in `lower_sim`

The memory ops are genuinely harder than arithmetic: they touch *user* memory, so correctness needs address-agreement (P1) **plus** the accessed bytes being off machine-private memory. I built it in stages, each committed and `[propext, Quot.sound]`:

### The reusable algebra — `MemFacts.lean` (new file, the memory analog of `SlotFacts`)
- **`loadWord_agree` / `loadWord_agree_off`** — the machine load equals the IL load: the addresses are literally equal (P1: `x2 ≡ sp`, so the slot-loaded base + imm = the IL address), and the bytes agree because they're off `MachPriv`.
- **`storeWord_mem_agree`** — the key insight: a store writes *identical bytes* into IL and machine memory, so it preserves agreement **pointwise** — no in-range/out-of-range case split.
- **`StInv_storeWord_user` / `StInv_storeByte_user`** — a user store off this activation's hole (⇒ disjoint from live slots) and off the blob (⇒ `Installed` survives) re-establishes `StInv`.
- **`range_disjoint_of_bytes`** — pointwise-off ⇒ `toNat` range-disjoint, proved constructively (no `by_contra`, which would pull `Classical`).

### The integration
- Added **`MemAccOff`** to `lower_sim` — the per-statement "accessed bytes off `MachPriv`" side condition, recursing through `seq` like `exec` (trivial for the non-memory cases), threaded through the seq case.
- **`ld`/`lbu`** go through the (mem-generalized) `single_op_sim`; **`sd`/`sb`** are two loads then the machine store via `StInv_store*_user`.
- `#guard`: `emit frameLocal.body` (which exercises `sd`+`ld`) == the real `Compile.lower`.

`lower_sim` now covers the **entire straight-line + memory fragment** (skip, annot, 6 arith ops, ld/sd/lbu/sb, seq). Full build green; the only remaining `lower_sim` `sorry` is control-flow (Phase 4.3, which needs an outcome-carrying generalization + the `Emitted` label-offset algebra).

A few gotchas worth flagging (saved to memory): `St.storeByte`/`loadByte` are `@[inline]` and won't unfold via `simp [name]` — needs full-name `unfold`; `StInv_store*_user`'s blob disjunction is the *opposite* order to `range_disjoint_of_bytes` (use `.symm`); the store no-wrap bound isn't derivable from off-`MachPriv` so `MemAccOff`'s `sd` case carries it explicitly; and `blobLen > 0` is recovered from the emitted store instruction itself.

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  19:03:05; tool 83ms

```
=== SymInstr + lower (control flow) ===
25:  Pipeline: lower to `SymInstr` (labels symbolic, calls by name) → lay out the
54:/-- Position-independent instruction stream: concrete instructions, label
55:    markers (emit nothing), and label/name-relative control transfers. -/
56:inductive SymInstr where
58:  | label (l : Nat)                            -- marker (0 bytes)
59:  | br    (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (l : Nat)     -- branch (phys regs) to label
60:  | jmp   (l : Nat)                            -- jal x0, label
72:def loadSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
78:def storeSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
82:/-- Fresh-label supply. -/
84:def fresh : M Nat := modifyGet fun n => (n, n + 1)
89:def synthConst (t : Reg) (v : Int) : List SymInstr :=
95:/-- Lower one statement. `brks`/`conts` are the enclosing block-end / loop-top
96:    label stacks (indexed exactly like `brkB`/`contL`'s de Bruijn indices);
97:    `epi` is this function's epilogue label. `wf` guarantees index ranges and
98:    call arities; out-of-range lookups fall back to label 0 (never emitted for
100:def lower (dat : Data) (brks conts : List Nat) (epi : Nat) : PStmt → M (List SymInstr)
103:  | .seq a b        => do pure ((← lower dat brks conts epi a) ++ (← lower dat brks conts epi b))
117:  | .ife c a b t e  => do
118:      let lT ← fresh; let lEnd ← fresh
119:      let et ← lower dat brks conts epi t
120:      let ee ← lower dat brks conts epi e
122:           ++ [.br c T0 T1 lT] ++ ee ++ [.jmp lEnd, .label lT] ++ et ++ [.label lEnd]
123:  | .while c a b body => do
124:      let lTop ← fresh; let lBody ← fresh; let lEnd ← fresh
125:      let eb ← lower dat brks (lTop :: conts) epi body
126:      pure <| [.label lTop] ++ loadSlot a T0 ++ loadSlot b T1
127:           ++ [.br c T0 T1 lBody, .jmp lEnd, .label lBody]
128:           ++ eb ++ [.jmp lTop, .label lEnd]
129:  | .block body     => do
130:      let lEnd ← fresh
131:      let eb ← lower dat (lEnd :: brks) conts epi body
132:      pure <| eb ++ [.label lEnd]
133:  | .brkB k         => pure [.jmp (brks.getD k 0)]
134:  | .contL k        => pure [.jmp (conts.getD k 0)]
135:  | .ret            => pure [.jmp epi]
```

=== machine branch/jump step (Rv64i) ===
  | .beq  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if s.rget rs1 = s.rget rs2 then s.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else next)
  | .blt  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if (s.rget rs1).slt (s.rget rs2) then s.pc + imm.signExtend 64 else next)
  | .bge  rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if (s.rget rs1).slt (s.rget rs2) then next else s.pc + imm.signExtend 64)
  | .bgeu rs1 rs2 imm =>
      s.setPc (if (s.rget rs1).ult (s.rget rs2) then next else s.pc + imm.signExtend 64)
  | .jal  rd imm => (s.rset rd next).setPc (s.pc + imm.signExtend 64)
  | .jalr rd rs1 imm =>
      let t := (s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64) &&& (~~~ (1 : Word))
      (s.rset rd next).setPc t
  | .unknown => s

/-- Run until pc = `halt`. `partial`: used only for executable validation,
    NOT in proofs (the proof side reasons about `step` directly). -/
partial def runUntil (halt : Word) (s : State) : State :=
  if s.pc = halt then s else runUntil halt (step s)

/-- Structural fuel-based runner: usable in proofs (`decide`/`native_decide`)
    because it is total. Stops early when `pc = halt`. -/
def runFuel (halt : Word) : Nat → State → State
  | 0,     s => s
  | n + 1, s => if s.pc = halt then s else runFuel halt n (step s)

/-- Steps actually taken before halting (or `fuel` if it never halts). -/
def stepsToHalt (halt : Word) : Nat → State → Nat
  | 0,     _ => 0
  | n + 1, s => if s.pc = halt then 0 else 1 + stepsToHalt halt n (step s)

end Rv64i

165	/-! ## The compiler: LowIR → flat RV64I
166	
167	    Structured control flow is lowered to the four trusted branches + `jal`. The
168	    per-instruction PC-simulation burden lives here (proved once), not in the
169	    per-program correctness proofs above.
170	
171	    Layouts (offsets are byte deltas relative to the branching instruction; each
172	    instruction is 4 bytes). Both use only the *positive* branch form, so the
173	    excluded `bne`/`bltu` are never needed:
174	
175	      ife c a b T E:                       while c a b BODY:
176	        0: branch c → THEN  (+4*(|E|+2))     0: branch c → BODY  (+8)
177	        1.. : compile E                      1: jal → END        (+4*(|B|+2))
178	        : jal → END        (+4*(|T|+1))      2.. : compile BODY
179	        : compile T                          : jal → TOP         (-4*(|B|+2))
180	        END:                                 END:
181	-/
182	
183	open Rv64i (Instr decode)
184	
185	/-- The branch instruction for a condition (positive form). -/
186	def condInstr (c : Cond) (a b : Reg) (off : Int) : Instr :=
187	  let imm : BitVec 13 := BitVec.ofInt 13 off
188	  match c with
189	  | .eq  => .beq  a b imm
190	  | .lt  => .blt  a b imm
191	  | .ge  => .bge  a b imm
192	  | .geu => .bgeu a b imm
193	
194	/-- Unconditional jump (`jal x0, off`). -/
195	def jal0 (off : Int) : Instr := .jal 0 (BitVec.ofInt 21 off)
196	
197	def compile : Stmt → List Instr
198	  | .skip            => []
199	  | .seq a b         => compile a ++ compile b
200	  | .addi rd rs imm  => [.addi rd rs imm]
201	  | .add  rd r1 r2   => [.add rd r1 r2]
202	  | .sub  rd r1 r2   => [.sub rd r1 r2]
203	  | .orr  rd r1 r2   => [.or rd r1 r2]
204	  | .slli rd rs sh   => [.slli rd rs sh]

   - **Axiom hygiene**: target `#print axioms X = [propext, Quot.sound]`. `omega` on compound goals (∧/↔/∨), `by_cases`, `decide_eq_decide`, stdlib `signExtend` lemmas, and `native_decide` all pull `Classical.choice`/`ofReduceBool` — avoided. Use `cases Nat.decEq`/`Nat.decLe`, constructive `Or.inl/inr` case walks, `rcases Nat.lt_or_ge`.
   - **StInv relation** (P1 frame-padding oracle makes it plain equality): 6 conjuncts — `m.rget 2 = s.sp`; slot invariant (`s.rget r = m.loadWord(sp+ofNat(slotOff r))` for 1≤r≤maxRegF); `Installed L m`; off-MachPriv memory agreement (`∀a, ¬MachPriv → s.mem a = m.mem a`); `holes.head? = some(s.sp, userOff fd)`; `s.sp.toNat%8=0`.
   - **MachPriv** = `memRange a codeBase blobLen ∨ ∃ h ∈ holes, memRange a h.1 h.2`. **memRange a base len** = `base.toNat ≤ a.toNat ∧ a.toNat < base.toNat + len`. **Layout.blobLen** = `segStart + (dataSegment data).length`.
   - **Compiler lowering** (Compile.lower): ld/lbu → `loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [mem-op T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0` (single_op_sim shape); sd/sb → `loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [mem-op T0 T1 imm]` (two loads, NO storeSlotI). slotOff r = 8*(1+r); userOff fd = 8*(maxRegF fd + 2). SP=2, T0=5, T1=6, RA=1, A i=10+i.
   - **P1 payoff for memory ops**: IL address = machine address literally (T0 loaded from rs's slot = s.rget rs). Correctness needs only that accessed bytes are off MachPriv.
   - **@[inline] gotcha**: `St.rget/rset/storeByte/loadByte` do NOT unfold via `simp [name]` (nor the opened alias with `unfold`); use FULL name `unfold LowIR.Prog.St.storeByte`. Concrete-index reads like `(s.rset 6 v).rget 6 = v` hold by `rfl` (defeq). `State.*` versions DO unfold via simp.
   - **exec_* unfolders** (ExecFacts.lean, namespace LowIR.Prog) take `(P) (dbase) (pad) (stackLo)` as EXPLICIT section variables, then fuel/regs/s.
   - **induction gotcha**: `induction fuel generalizing stmt s s' m pos` reverts into `ih` ONLY the hyps depending on generalized vars (hexec/hinv/hpc/hem/hreg/hnw/hbd/haccess); hframe/hseg/hblob stay AMBIENT (omit from ih calls).
   - `stepN` (own iteration; f^[n]/Nat.iterate are Mathlib-only), `stepN_add`, `pc_congr`/`pc_add4`.

     theorem range_disjoint_of_bytes (addr base : Word) (len : Nat)
         (hlen : 0 < len) (hwa : addr.toNat + 8 ≤ 2 ^ 64)
         (hoff : ∀ i, i < 8 → ¬ memRange (addr + BitVec.ofNat 64 i) base len) :
         addr.toNat + 8 ≤ base.toNat ∨ base.toNat + len ≤ addr.toNat := by
       have key : ∀ i, i < 8 →
           ¬ (base.toNat ≤ addr.toNat + i ∧ addr.toNat + i < base.toNat + len) := by
         intro i hi hc
         refine hoff i hi ?_
         have ht : (addr + BitVec.ofNat 64 i).toNat = addr.toNat + i := by
           simp only [BitVec.toNat_add, BitVec.toNat_ofNat, Nat.reducePow]; omega
         unfold memRange; rw [ht]; exact hc
       rcases Nat.lt_or_ge (addr.toNat + 8) (base.toNat + 1) with h | h
       · exact Or.inl (by omega)
       rcases Nat.lt_or_ge addr.toNat (base.toNat + len) with h2 | h2
       · exfalso
         rcases Nat.lt_or_ge addr.toNat base.toNat with h3 | h3
         · exact key (base.toNat - addr.toNat) (by omega) ⟨by omega, by omega⟩
         · exact key 0 (by omega) ⟨by omega, by omega⟩
       · exact Or.inr (by omega)
     ```

def step (s : State) : State :=
  let next := s.pc + 4
  match decode (fetch32 s) with
  | .addi rd rs1 imm => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64)).setPc next
  | .add  rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 + s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .sub  rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 - s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .or   rd rs1 rs2 => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 ||| s.rget rs2)).setPc next
  | .slli rd rs1 sh  => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 <<< sh)).setPc next
  | .srli rd rs1 sh  => (s.rset rd (s.rget rs1 >>> sh)).setPc next
  | .lbu  rd rs1 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.rset rd ((s.loadByte a).setWidth 64)).setPc next
  | .ld   rd rs1 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.rset rd (s.loadWord a)).setPc next
  | .sb   rs1 rs2 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64
      (s.storeByte a ((s.rget rs2).setWidth 8)).setPc next
  | .sd   rs1 rs2 imm =>
      let a := s.rget rs1 + imm.signExtend 64

43:inductive Cond where
83:@[inline] def evalCond (c : Cond) (x y : Word) : Bool :=
inductive Cond where
  | eq        -- ==        → beq
  | lt        -- <  signed → blt
  | ge        -- >= signed → bge
  | geu       -- >= unsigned → bgeu
deriving DecidableEq, Repr
=== evalCond ===
@[inline] def evalCond (c : Cond) (x y : Word) : Bool :=
  match c with
  | .eq  => x = y
  | .lt  => x.slt y
  | .ge  => !x.slt y
  | .geu => !x.ult y
```

I worked through the control-flow ops in dependency order. Here's where they stand.

## Control flow — foundation + the three leaf ops done

The control-flow ops split cleanly into **leaf transfers** (a single jump — provable now) and **compound ops** (they wrap arbitrary sub-statements — need a framework first). I did the leaves.

### Foundation (all `[propext, Quot.sound]`)
- **Machine steps** — `step_jal`, `step_beq`, `step_blt`, `step_bge`, `step_bgeu` (the `jal x0` and the four positive-form branches the compiler actually emits).
- **`signExtend_ofInt_21` / `_13`** — a signed offset `δ = target − here` (within `resolveOne`'s emit guard) sign-extends to itself. This was the one piece I was unsure would stay `Classical`-free — the stdlib `signExtend` lemmas are tainted here — but the `toInt = bmod` route works: `rw [Int.bmod_def]; split <;> omega` closes the `bmod` identity at the concrete modulus.
- **`jump_lands`** — a PC-relative transfer from `codeBase + here` by `δ` lands at `codeBase + target`, forward or backward, wrap-safe in `BitVec 64`.

### The ops (one by one)
- **`jump_sim`** — the leaf unconditional jump: preserves `StInv` (jumps don't mutate IL state; `StInv_scratch` on x0) and lands at `codeBase + target`.
- **`ret_sim`**, **`brkB_sim`**, **`contL_sim`** — the three leaf control-transfer ops, each `jump_sim` plus extracting `s' = s` from `exec_ret`/`exec_brkB`/`exec_contL`. The resolved target (epilogue / k-th break / k-th continue label position) is a parameter, supplied later by the label environment.

Committed in two steps (`d766ba1` foundation, `7993131` leaf ops), full build green.

### The compound three (`ife`/`while`/`block`) — deliberately not attempted yet
These can't be done "one by one" independently: each embeds an arbitrary sub-statement that can produce *any* outcome (a `break` inside an `if` inside a `while`...), so they all require the same prerequisite — an **outcome-carrying `lower_sim`** whose conclusion carries the `Outcome` and lands at `codeBase + landPos(outcome)`, threading a label environment. I've written up the design (parameterize by an abstract label→position map with a consistency hypothesis that Phase 2's layout discharges — this decouples the simulation proof from the layout arithmetic) in `RESUME-PROGSIM.md` and memory. All the atoms it needs — the branch/jump steps, the offset arithmetic, `jump_sim` — are now in place, so that's a clean next build rather than a from-scratch one.

I stopped at the leaf/compound boundary rather than start the framework mid-turn, since it's a substantial generalization best done as its own focused unit (and starting it half-way risks a broken state). Want me to build the outcome-carrying framework and do `block` (the simplest compound) next?

def layoutItems : List SymInstr → Nat → List (Nat × SymInstr) × List (Nat × Nat) × Nat
  | [], pos => ([], [], pos)
  | .label l :: rest, pos =>
      let (flat, lbls, pos') := layoutItems rest pos
      ((pos, .label l) :: flat, (l, pos) :: lbls, pos')
  | si :: rest, pos =>
      let (flat, lbls, pos') := layoutItems rest (pos + symSize si)
      ((pos, si) :: flat, lbls, pos')

/-- Pass A: walk the whole program, assigning byte positions. Returns the flat
    positioned stream, the label→addr map, the function→addr map, and the end
    position (total code bytes). -/
def layout : List (Name × List SymInstr) →
    (start : Nat) →
    List (Nat × SymInstr) × List (Nat × Nat) × List (Name × Nat) × Nat
  | [], pos => ([], [], [], pos)
  | (n, items) :: rest, pos =>
      let (flat1, lbls1, pos') := layoutItems items pos
      let (flat2, lbls2, fns, endP) := layout rest pos'
      (flat1 ++ flat2, lbls1 ++ lbls2, (n, pos) :: fns, endP)

    with NO auipc, staying inside the 16-encoding surface and keeping the blob

**The compiler already computes the mapping.** `layoutItems` returns exactly a label→position table — `List (Nat × Nat)` of `(l, pos)` pairs — threaded up through `layout`. `resolveOne` then consumes it to turn each `.jmp l` / `.br c a b l` into a concrete instruction with offset `tgt − here`. So the raw data you're describing already exists inside the pipeline; the open question is purely how the *proof* consumes it.

**We already do exactly this for straight-line code.** My `emit : PStmt → List Instr` *is* a compiler-emitted "IR → concrete instructions" mapping, and `Emitted L pos (emit stmt)` is the check that the mapping matches what's actually installed — `#guard`'d against the real `lower` for `sub3`/`frameLocal`. So your idea isn't a new paradigm; it's "generalize `emit` from straight-line to position/label-aware, and feed it the label table." That's continuous with the existing architecture, which is a point in its favor — low conceptual risk.

**What it buys us.** The genuinely painful part of the control-flow proof is the two-pass layout arithmetic: fresh-label positions are sums of sub-block sizes, and jumps encode `tgt − here`. A structured mapping (a `Trace` that mirrors the IR tree, annotating each node with its start position and its fresh-label positions) lets the semantic induction *read positions off the node* instead of re-deriving them from `symSize` sums. All the semantic atoms this needs — `jump_sim`, the branch steps, the offset arithmetic — are already done. So it moves the layout bookkeeping out of the semantic induction and into one separate faithfulness lemma. Real de-risking.

**The one thing that decides the whole shape** — and where I need your call:

`prog_sim` as written is **fully general** (`∀ P entry fd args …`), and it already takes the *real* layout as `hL : layoutOf P entry L.codeBase = some L` plus `Installed L m0` pinning the real bytes. That has a consequence: **the mapping can't be a trusted compiler output.** It has to be *derived from / proven consistent with* `L`. So:

- **If we keep the general theorem:** faithfulness ("the trace's resolved instructions = the slice of `L.instrs` at that node") is a *structural lemma over `layout`/`layoutItems`/`resolveOne`* — proved once by induction. More work, but it discharges `prog_sim` as stated.
- **If we retarget to the actual binaries** (hex0/hex1/strlen — the real bootstrap goal): faithfulness becomes a *per-program `decide`* against the concrete stream, matching the existing differential-oracle / `#guard` style. Much easier — but it only covers those programs, and plain `decide` on hundreds of instructions may be slow while `native_decide` would taint the axiom set (`ofReduceBool`), which the campaign has been careful to avoid.

These aren't mutually exclusive — the *semantic* core (`lower_sim_cf`: exec-outcome ↔ machine-pc) is general and identical either way; only the layout-side discharge differs.

So my question before I design: **is the goal still the general compiler-correctness `prog_sim`, or is it really "prove the specific compiled hex0/hex1 binaries correct"?** If it's the binaries, the mapping-as-checked-witness is clearly the right call and gets much simpler. If it's the general theorem, I'd still build the structured trace (for the induction-lineup benefit) but faithfulness stays a structural lemma rather than a `decide`.

Agreed on the goal, and I think your intuition is right for a reason that's worth making precise — because it also tells us *how* to build the mapping so it actually pays off.

**Why a validated mapping genuinely helps the *general* proof (not just ergonomics).** The hard part of general control-flow compiler correctness is induction-hypothesis strength. Two things have to compose across control-flow joins: the *semantics* (an inner `break`/`ret` lands somewhere non-local) and the *positions* (a node's code occupies `[p, p+size)` with internal label positions that are sums of sub-block sizes). The outcome-carrying generalization handles the semantic side. But if you *also* make the semantic induction carry all the position-composition invariants, the IH gets heavy and that's exactly where these proofs stall. A validated mapping is **strengthening-by-precomputation**: it factors the positional layout into a separate, *semantics-free* artifact, so the semantic IH only ever talks about `exec`-outcome ↔ machine-(pc, regs, mem), with positions handed in as parameters read off the mapping node. You're not avoiding the layout arithmetic; you're quarantining it into one structural lemma so it never entangles the semantic recursion. That's a real reason it helps the general case, and it matches how the tractable control-flow compiler proofs in the literature are structured.

**"Validated" is the load-bearing word, and `prog_sim` already forces it to be sound.** `prog_sim` takes the real layout as `hL : layoutOf P entry … = some L` and pins the real bytes via `Installed L m0`. So the mapping can't be a trusted side-output — it has to be *derived from / proven equal to* what `layout`/`layoutItems`/`resolveOne` actually produced. The cleanest form of "validated" here is: the mapping is a **certified view of the layout** — each node's recorded start position and fresh-label positions are *provably* the real byte positions (a structural lemma over `layoutItems`), and each jump's offset is *provably* `tgt − here`. Then it's faithful by construction, not by cross-check.

**The proof-transfer angle is the strongest argument, and it changes the design.** If the mapping is only an *internal* device for proving `prog_sim`, it's just the quarantine trick above. But if you **expose it as a first-class relation** — "this IR node ↔ this assembly region + these labels" — then it's a reusable refinement witness that *any* IR-level property can be transported along, not just this one correctness statement. That's what makes it worth more than the proof it first unlocks: the same validated correspondence lets you carry future IR-level facts (a second correctness property, a size/WCET bound, a security invariant) down to the assembly without re-deriving the layout each time. So I'd design the mapping to be a *stated relation in the API*, not a `let` buried inside `lower_sim_cf`.

**The one real fork — where the mapping is produced.** There's a coupling to resolve: `lower` allocates fresh labels via a stateful counter in `M`, so the mapping must know which label ids belong to which IR node. Two ways:

1. **Proof-side reconstruction** — a `traceOf`/`lowerWithTrace` in the proof layer that mirrors `lower`'s allocation, with a lemma `lowerWithTrace.fst = lower`. TCB-neutral (the executable compiler is untouched and stays validated by the differential oracle + QEMU), at the cost of re-deriving the allocation once.
2. **Compiler emits it** — `lower`/`layout` return the structured trace alongside the stream. Authoritative (the same fold that lays out the code produces the mapping, so faithfulness is nearly definitional), but it enlarges the executable and forces re-validation, and `prog_sim` would have to thread the trace through its hypotheses.

I lean toward a hybrid: define `lowerWithTrace` proof-side so allocation and mapping are produced *together* (you get the lockstep for free, no reconstructing `fresh`'s behavior), but prove `lowerWithTrace.fst = lower` so nothing in the trusted path changes. Best of both — authoritative construction, zero TCB growth.

Your call on that fork is the thing I'd want settled before I design the `Trace` type. Do you want it kept out of the executable compiler (my lean: yes), and do you want the correspondence *exposed in the API* for transfer (my lean: yes — it's most of the value)?

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  19:30:14; model 1m04s

> 💭 (thinking)
> 

Yes — but in a precise sense worth pinning down, because it tells you what to expose.

**What the trace already is, in DWARF terms.** Two of the three things debug info carries fall straight out:

- **Line table (address → source location).** Our node↔region map *is* an address→IR-node table. Given a PC, the node whose region contains it is the "current source line." The non-overlap/tiling property (which we need for the proof anyway) is exactly what makes that reverse lookup a *function* — every PC in exactly one node. So backtraces and "breakpoint at this IR statement" work.
- **Variable locations.** This is already sitting in `StInv`: each IR register lives at `slotOff r` in the frame (or *is* the machine SP). That's precisely DWARF's "variable at fbreg+offset," and `StInv` *proves* it holds at every related state.

**And here's the twist that makes it more than ordinary debug info: it's *validated*.** Real DWARF is best-effort and famously lies under optimization ("value optimized out," wrong line). A trace we've proven faithful to `layout`/`resolveOne` is debug info you can *trust* — a breakpoint set at an IR node provably lands at the right PC. Even stronger on the variable side: we don't just get "x is at slot k," we get — via `StInv` + `prog_sim` — "slot k *holds x's current abstract value*." That's a value-level correspondence standard debug info doesn't give you. So it's not merely "works as debug info," it's *verified* debug info, which is a genuinely hard thing to produce and mostly a research topic.

**Three honest caveats so we don't overclaim:**

1. **It's clean because we're -O0.** Memory-locals lowering is what makes each IR node map to a contiguous region and each register to a fixed slot. Add register allocation, reordering, or DCE and the relation stops being "node ↔ contiguous range" — one node scatters across instructions, locations become PC-dependent (DWARF location *lists*). The trace type would have to grow from "region + slots" toward a genuine range/location-list relation. Fine, but it means "serves as debug info" is a property of the current lowering, not free forever.

2. **Proof artifact ≠ serialized blob.** The trace is a Lean structure the *proof* consumes. To hand it to an actual debugger you'd serialize it (even a flat table), and *that* step is outside the proof — a verified structure plus an unverified emitter gives you only "debug info derived from a verified mapping," not "verified debug info." If you want the latter, the emitted format has to be the thing you state the faithfulness lemma about (or you prove the emitter faithful). Worth deciding later, not now.

3. **Direction and granularity.** Debuggers want PC→node (reverse) and often per-instruction; we naturally have node→region (forward). Both are the same relation, but the debug consumer wants the reverse projection as a total function with a uniqueness proof.

**The design implication, which is the useful part:** debug-info-view and proof-transfer-view are both just *read-only queries over the same validated relation* — `nodeAtPC`, `slotOfReg`, `regionOfNode`. That's the argument for building the trace with a small **query API** and proving its structural properties (totality, uniqueness, region-disjointness) up front, rather than inlining lookups into `lower_sim_cf`. Those same proven queries are what `prog_sim`'s induction reads positions from, what a future property transfers along, *and* what a debug emitter would serialize. One substrate, three consumers.

So I'd fold "could be debug info" into the design as: **expose the trace as a relation with a queryable, property-carrying API** — which we already wanted for transfer. It doesn't add work; it just confirms the API should be the deliverable, not an internal helper. The only debug-specific extra (serialization + a faithful emitter) I'd defer until there's an actual debugger consumer.

  | contL  (k : Nat)                               -- restart k-th enclosing loop

### 🛠️ **Tool result**  ·  19:49:53; tool 1.6s

```
=== PStmt (Prog.lean) ===
=== FunDef + Program structures ===
structure FunDef where
  argc      : Nat
  rvc       : Nat
  params    : Vector Reg argc
  rets      : Vector Reg rvc
  frameSize : Nat
  frameReg  : Reg
  body      : Stmt

abbrev Env := List (Name × FunDef)

/-- The program's read-only data segment: named constant byte objects,
    referenced from code as slices via `cref` (address) + `clen` (length). -/
abbrev Data := List (Name × List Byte)

/-- A whole program: functions plus const data. Where the data objects LIVE
    is not part of the program — the semantics takes a base map (`dbase`),
    ∀-quantifiable like D8's `sp₀`, so programs stay address-independent. -/
structure Program where
  env  : Env
  data : Data

/-- Data-free view (keeps pre-data call sites unchanged). -/
instance : Coe Env Program := ⟨fun env => ⟨env, []⟩⟩

/-- IL machine state: register file (x0 hardwired 0), byte memory, and the
    semantic stack pointer — no instruction writes `sp`; only call/return move it. -/
structure St where
  regs : Reg → Word
  mem  : Word → Byte
  sp   : Word

@[inline] def St.rget (s : St) (i : Reg) : Word := if i = 0 then 0 else s.regs i

@[inline] def St.rset (s : St) (i : Reg) (v : Word) : St :=
  if i = 0 then s else { s with regs := fun j => if j = i then v else s.regs j }

@[inline] def St.loadByte (s : St) (a : Word) : Byte := s.mem a

@[inline] def St.storeByte (s : St) (a : Word) (b : Byte) : St :=
  { s with mem := fun x => if x = a then b else s.mem x }

/-- Little-endian 64-bit load — byte order identical to `Rv64i.State.loadWord`. -/
def St.loadWord (s : St) (a : Word) : Word :=
  (s.mem a).setWidth 64 |||
  ((s.mem (a + 1)).setWidth 64) <<< 8 |||
```

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  19:54:55; model 14.9s

Done. The dumper is written, working, and committed (`4419db4`).

## `LowIR/Dump.lean` — WAST-style s-expr dumper

It renders a `Prog.Program` / `FunDef` / `Stmt` as nested s-expressions in a WebAssembly-text idiom. Here's `strlen`:

```wast
(func $strlen (param $r10) (result $r12) (frame 0 $r3)
  (local.set $r7 (i64.add (local.get $r0) (i64.const 1)))
  (local.set $r5 (i64.add (local.get $r10) (i64.const 0)))
  (local.set $r6 (i64.load8_u offset=0 (local.get $r5)))
  (while (i64.ge_u (local.get $r6) (local.get $r7))
    (local.set $r5 (i64.add (local.get $r5) (i64.const 1)))
    (local.set $r6 (i64.load8_u offset=0 (local.get $r5))))
  (local.set $r12 (i64.sub (local.get $r5) (local.get $r10))))
```

And the interesting structure survives cleanly — `strtoull`'s `block`/`while`/`brk`:

```wast
(block
  (while (i64.lt_s (local.get $r0) (local.get $r16))
    (local.set $r7 (i64.load8_u offset=0 (local.get $r5)))
    (if (i64.ge_u (local.get $r7) (local.get $r20))
      (then …)
      (else
        (brk 0)))
    …))
```

**Design choices** that matter for where this is headed:
- **Seq spine flattened** into consecutive lines (not nested `(seq …)`) — reads like a real function body.
- **Closing parens attach to the last child line** (idiomatic Lisp), so the recursion is just `dumpStmt (n+1) child ++ ")"`.
- **`brk`/`cont` show their de-Bruijn depth** (`(brk 0)`), which is precisely the control-flow info the layout/label mapping will need to resolve.
- **`cref $hi` / `clen $hex0src` are `$name` references** that visibly point at the `(data $hi "\48\69\21\00")` decls — the IR↔data correspondence, rendered.

It's deliberately **not verified** (no parsing/round-trip — as you said, out of scope), and it's **standalone** (not in default build, since the demo `#eval`s would spam ~55 KB into the build log — run it with `lake env lean LowIR/Dump.lean`).

The reason I structured it as a per-node recursion with an indent parameter rather than a flat printer: this is the natural skeleton for the **trace** we discussed. Right now each `dumpStmt n node` case knows its node and its depth; the next step is to thread a byte-position (and resolved label positions for `if`/`while`/`block`) through the same recursion, so the same walk that pretty-prints the tree also emits the position annotation — one traversal serving the dump, the proof-transfer mapping, and eventually the debug-info emitter. Want me to take that next step (add positions to the walk), or iterate on the dump format first?

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:03:34; model 25.6s

> 💭 (thinking)

Verified against source. The serialization is **faithful** — but you've spotted a real format problem.

**It's correct data, ambiguous rendering.** `strlenF` declares `frameSize := 0, frameReg := 3`, and my `dumpFun` emits `(frame {frameSize} {frameReg})` → `(frame 0 $r3)`. So the `0` is the frame *size* and `$r3` is the frame *base register*. But written as `(frame 0 $r3)`, the leading bare `0` reads exactly like a register (r0) sitting in the base slot — which is precisely how you parsed it. The two operands have no labels saying which is size and which is base.

**Why `$r3` also looks disconnected:** for the size-0 functions (`strlen`/`strtoull`/`hex0`, all `frameReg := 3`) there *is* no frame, so the base register is a vestigial declared default the body never touches — you won't find a single `ld`/`sd` against `$r3`. It only becomes real for the functions with an actual frame: `hex1 (frame 1024 $r8)`, `main (frame 96 $r8)`, `cmain (frame 16 $r8)` — and there `$r8` *is* used as the load/store base.

Let me relabel so it can't be misread:

**🔧 tool → Edit**  ·  60ms
```json

```

### 🤖 **Assistant**  ·  20:04:08; model 8.2s

To answer your question directly: **the serialization was correct, but the format was misleading.** `(frame 0 $r3)` faithfully encoded `frameSize := 0, frameReg := 3` — the `0` was the size, `$r3` the base register — but with no labels the leading `0` read like the base register itself (r0), which is what you saw. Fixed to `(frame (size 0) (base $r3))` (committed `f008d73`).

Two things the digging confirmed, worth keeping in mind for the trace work:
- **Every function's frame fields render faithfully** — I checked all six against source: `strlen`/`strtoull`/`hex0` are `(size 0)(base $r3)`, `hex1`/`main`/`cmain` are `(size 1024/96/16)(base $r8)`.
- **`base` is only meaningful when `size > 0`.** For the three size-0 functions, `$r3` is a vestigial default — the body has no `ld`/`sd` against it. When we thread layout positions onto this structure, the frame `base`/`size` is exactly the anchor for the `StInv` slot invariant (`slotOff r` off the base), so the size-0 case is where "no spilled locals" will show up as an empty slot region. Good to have surfaced it now rather than in the proof.

```
=== loadSlot / storeSlot / symSize ===
def loadSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
  if r = 0 then [.ins (.addi t 0 0)]
  else [.ins (.ld t SP (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r)))]

def storeSlot (r : Reg) (t : Reg) : List SymInstr :=
  if r = 0 then []
  else [.ins (.sd SP t (BitVec.ofNat 12 (slotOff r)))]

def symSize : SymInstr → Nat
  | .label _ => 0
  | .cref _  => 20
  | _        => 4

=== lower: arith + mem cases (to compare vs emit) ===
def lower (dat : Data) (brks conts : List Nat) (epi : Nat) : PStmt → M (List SymInstr)
  | .skip           => pure []
  | .annot _        => pure []
  | .seq a b        => do pure ((← lower dat brks conts epi a) ++ (← lower dat brks conts epi b))
  | .addi rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.addi T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .add  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
                              ++ [.ins (.add T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .sub  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
                              ++ [.ins (.sub T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .orr  rd r1 r2  => pure <| loadSlot r1 T0 ++ loadSlot r2 T1
                              ++ [.ins (.or T0 T0 T1)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .slli rd rs sh  => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.slli T0 T0 sh)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .srli rd rs sh  => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.srli T0 T0 sh)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .lbu  rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.lbu T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .ld   rd rs imm => pure <| loadSlot rs T0 ++ [.ins (.ld T0 T0 imm)] ++ storeSlot rd T0
  | .sb   rb rv imm => pure <| loadSlot rb T0 ++ loadSlot rv T1 ++ [.ins (.sb T0 T1 imm)]
  | .sd   rb rv imm => pure <| loadSlot rb T0 ++ loadSlot rv T1 ++ [.ins (.sd T0 T1 imm)]
```

```
def emit : PStmt → List Instr
  | .skip            => []
  | .annot _         => []
  | .addi rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.addi T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .add  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.add T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .sub  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.sub T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .orr  rd r1 r2   => loadSlotI r1 T0 ++ loadSlotI r2 T1 ++ [.or  T0 T0 T1] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .slli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.slli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .srli rd rs sh   => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.srli T0 T0 sh] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .lbu  rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.lbu T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .ld   rd rs imm  => loadSlotI rs T0 ++ [.ld  T0 T0 imm] ++ storeSlotI rd T0
  | .sb   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sb T0 T1 imm]
  | .sd   rb rv imm  => loadSlotI rb T0 ++ loadSlotI rv T1 ++ [.sd T0 T1 imm]
  | .seq a b         => emit a ++ emit b
  | _                => []

/-! ## `Emitted` — the static "code segment carries `is` at `pos`" fact. -/

/-- `L.instrs` has `is` as its segment starting at byte offset `pos`
    (`pos` 4-aligned; each `is[j]` is `L.instrs[pos/4 + j]`). Static in `L`; the
    machine fetch is recovered from `Installed` (a `StInv` conjunct). -/
def Emitted (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is : List Instr) : Prop :=
  pos % 4 = 0 ∧ ∀ (j : Nat) (h : j < is.length),
    ∃ (h2 : pos / 4 + j < L.instrs.length), L.instrs[pos / 4 + j] = is[j]'h

/-- `Emitted` of a concatenation splits: `is₁` at `pos`, `is₂` at `pos + 4|is₁|`. -/
theorem Emitted_append_left (L : Layout) (pos : Nat) (is₁ is₂ : List Instr)
    (h : Emitted L pos (is₁ ++ is₂)) : Emitted L pos is₁ := by
  obtain ⟨ha, hf⟩ := h
  refine ⟨ha, fun j hj => ?_⟩
  obtain ⟨h2, he⟩ := hf j (by simp only [List.length_append]; omega)
  exact ⟨h2, by rw [he, List.getElem_append_left hj]⟩
=== condInstr/jal0 namespace, T0/T1 ===
47:def T0 : Reg := 5
48:def T1 : Reg := 6
```

IR verification advanced with a concrete, validated milestone.

## `emitCF` — the validated label-aware lowering

This is the piece the compound control-flow proof was blocked on: `emit` is label-free and stubs the control-flow ops with `[]`, so the current `lower_sim` literally can't state them. `emitCF` resolves them — it's the position/label-aware extension that produces exactly what the real `Compile.lower ▸ layoutItems ▸ resolveOne` pipeline emits:

- Every `.jmp`/`.br` → a single `jal x0`/branch at offset `target − here`; every label → 0 bytes.
- Straight-line cases **delegate to `emit`** (so the existing faithfulness carries over for free).
- The `ife`/`while` internal offsets are **position-independent** (size-relative via `csize`); only `ret`/`brkB`/`contL` targets (external labels) depend on `here`. That's a nice structural fact — it means the tricky layout arithmetic is confined to three leaf ops.
- `csize` + `emitCF_length : (emitCF …).length = csize`, `[propext, Quot.sound]`.

**The validation is the real payoff** — this *is* the "validated mapping" we designed. `#guard realResolve == emitCF` for `strlen`/`strtoull`/`hex0`/`hex1`, covering `while`, `block`, `ife`, `brkB`, `contL`, `ret`, and all their nestings, decidably against the trusted compiler.

And it earned its keep immediately: the first-cut harness passed strlen/strtoull but **failed hex0**. Rather than assume `emitCF` was wrong, I diffed — lengths matched (357=357, so `csize` was right), only `jal` offsets differed, and they were all `.ret`s (`err code = seq (lit 14 code) .ret`). The bug was my *harness*: I'd collided the epilogue label with the first fresh label and pointed `epiPos` at the function start instead of the body's end. Fixed by planting the epilogue at `4·csize body` with `fresh` seeded clear of it — and now all four pass. Exactly the "the check catches structural errors before the proof does" benefit we wanted from making the mapping explicit.

**Next** (the remaining control-flow verification): the outcome-carrying `lower_sim` — conclusion carries the `Outcome` and lands at `codeBase + landPos(outcome)` — inducting over `emitCF`, with `block` the first compound case. All the atoms (`jump_sim`, branch steps, offset arithmetic, and now `emitCF`/`csize`) are in place.
